Proposition 13

(Validity)
.

If
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\text{ThyBB}
and
v\in\mathsf{Value}
then

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{bcst}(v)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v).

Proof 4.17
.

We reason using weak modus ponens (Prop.
2
(
4
)).
Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{bcst}(v)
.
By Lemma
4.12
(
2
)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{echo}(v)
,
and by Lemma
4.15
(
1
)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(v)
.
By Lemma
4.12
(
3
)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{ready}(v)
, and by Lemma
4.15
(
2
)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v)
.
By Lemma
4.12
(
4
)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)
as required.

4.2.3
Consistency & No duplication: “if correct
p
delivers
v
and correct (possibly equal)
p^{\prime}
delivers
v^{\prime}
then
v=v^{\prime}
”

Lemma 4.18
.

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}{\mathsf{(BrCorrect)}}
(in particular, it suffices that
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\text{ThyBB}
).
Then for
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Value}
:

(1)

If
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v)
then
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{ready}(v)
.

(2)

If
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(v^{\prime})
then
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{echo}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{echo}(v^{\prime}))
.

(3)

If
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v^{\prime})
then
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{ready}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{ready}(v^{\prime}))
.

Proof 4.19
.

We consider each part in turn:

(1)

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v)
.
From
{\mathsf{(BrCorrect)}}
also
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{ready}(v)
.
Using Theorem
2.13

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{ready}(v)
and so
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{ready}(v)
as required.

(2)

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(v^{\prime})
.
From
{\mathsf{(BrCorrect)}}
we have
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}]
.
Using Theorem
2.13
and Lemma
2.10
(
3
) (or just direct from the 3-twined property in Definition
5
)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{echo}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{echo}(v^{\prime}))
as required.

(3)

As for the proof of part
2
, noting that
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{ready}]
from
{\mathsf{(BrCorrect)}}
.

Proposition 14

(Consistency & No Duplication)
.

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\text{ThyBB}
and
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Value}
.
Then

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists_{01}$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v).

Proof 4.20
.

By Proposition
9
(
1
) it suffices to show for every
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Value}
that

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v^{\prime})
implies
v=v^{\prime}
.
So suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)
and
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v^{\prime})
.
By
{\mathsf{(BrDeliver?)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v)
and
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v^{\prime})
, so by Lemma
4.18
(
3
)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{ready}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{ready}(v^{\prime}))
.
By
{\mathsf{(BrReady?)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(v)
and
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(v^{\prime})
, so by Lemma
4.18
(
2
)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{echo}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{echo}(v^{\prime}))
.
It follows using
{\mathsf{(BrEcho01)}}
and Proposition
9
(
5
) that
v=v^{\prime}
as required.

, as required.

4.2.5
Totality: if correct
p
delivers
v
, then every correct
p^{\prime}
delivers
v

Proposition 16

(Totality)
.

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\text{ThyBB}
and
v\in\mathsf{Value}
.
Then:

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)\ \mathbin{\rightarrow}\ \Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v).

Proof 4.22
.

We reason using weak modus ponens (Prop.
2
(
4
)).
Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)
.
By
{\mathsf{(BrDeliver?)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v)
, so by Lemma
4.18
(
1
)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{ready}(v)
.
Using
{\mathsf{(BrReady!!)}}
(applied at every point)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{ready}(v)
, so by Lemma
4.15
(
2
)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(v)
.
Using
{\mathsf{(BrDeliver!)}}
(applied at every point)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)
.

Remark 4.23
.

Let us take a moment to unpack the statement of Totality in Proposition
16
and remember what the symbols mean.
As per Figure
4
this asserts that
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)
has truth-value
{\mathbf{t}}
or
{\mathbf{b}}
for every
p\in\mathsf{P}
.
The choice of
p
clearly does not matter here because both parts of the implication are modal.
Unpacking the truth-table of
\mathbin{\rightarrow}
from Figure
1
, it suffices to show that if the left-hand side has truth-value
{\mathbf{t}}
then the right-hand side has truth-value
{\mathbf{t}}
or
{\mathbf{b}}
.

So suppose
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}={\mathbf{t}}
, indicating that some honest participant delivers
v
.
The proof of Proposition
16
then shows that:
every other honest participant delivers
v
;
for any dishonest participants
\mathsf{dlvr}(v)
has truth-value
{\mathbf{b}}
;
and therefore, the truth-value of
\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{dlvr}(v)
is
{\mathbf{t}}
or (if dishonest participants are involved)
{\mathbf{b}}
.

The statement and proof of Proposition
16
do not reason explicitly on correct or byzantine participants.
They do not need to: this detail is all packaged neatly into how the modalities and implication work.
The proof is routine, following the structure of the axioms and applying lemmas in a natural way.

It is easy to take this magic for granted: how logic turns reasoning into (almost) routine symbolic manipulation.
But of course, this is why logical specifications can be so helpful.
Declarative logical specifications of the kind we see above are particularly powerful, because of how concise they can be.

4.3
Further discussion of the axioms

We take a moment to return to the axioms in Figure
6
, to discuss some design alternatives.

Axioms
{\mathsf{(BrDeliver!)}}
and
{\mathsf{(BrReady!)}}
in Figure
6
are slightly stronger than absolutely necessary.
In the presence of the other axioms, it suffices to assume
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{ready}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.\mathsf{dlvr}(a)
and
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.\mathsf{ready}(a)
.
It then is not hard to use the backwards rules to derive the stronger forms of the axioms as presented in Figure
6
.

The same is not true of
{\mathsf{(BrReady!!)}}
.
A weaker axiom
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{ready}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.\mathsf{ready}(a)
would
not
suffice and the stronger form of the axiom cannot be derived from it.

Conversely, changing
{\mathsf{(BrEcho!)}}
to
\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{bcst}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\mathsf{echo}(a)
would be wrong.
Consider the case of a byzantine participant who (dishonestly; contrary to the protocol) broadcasts
v
and
v^{\prime}
.
In our logic this would give
\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{bcst}(v)
and
\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{bcst}(v^{\prime})
truth-value
{\mathbf{b}}
.
According to the truth-table for
\mathbin{\rightarrow}
in Figure
1
, by our modified
{\mathsf{(BrEcho!)}}
rule,
\mathsf{echo}(v)
and
\mathsf{echo}(v^{\prime})
would then have to have truth-value
{\mathbf{b}}
or
{\mathbf{t}}
.
For an honest participant this would mean that both
\mathsf{echo}(v)
and
\mathsf{echo}(v^{\prime})
would have to have truth-value
{\mathbf{t}}
.
This would contradict
{\mathsf{(BrEcho01)}}
, which implies that no honest participant may echo more than one value.

Clause
3
of Remark
4.1
renders into logic as a forward rule
(\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(a)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{ready}(a))\mathbin{\rightarrow}\mathsf{ready}(a)
.
This rule appears more-or-less verbatim in Figure
6
as
{\mathsf{(BrReady!)}}
and
{\mathsf{(BrReady!!)}}
.
Yet in Figure
6
we only have the backward rule
{\mathsf{(BrReady?)}}
as
\mathsf{ready}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(a)
.
Why not a rule
{\mathsf{(BrReady?^{\prime})}}
of the form
\mathsf{ready}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}(a)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{ready}(a))
?
Algorithmically this is inefficient, because if somebody sends a ready message then by an inductive argument a quorum of echoes must exist.
In the logic,
{\mathsf{(BrReady?^{\prime})}}
would actually be
too weak
; it would permit a model in which every participant does
\mathsf{ready}(v)
, and for each
p

{\mathsf{(BrReady?^{\prime})}}
would be valid at
p
because of the contraquorum of all the
other

p^{\prime}
who also do
\mathsf{ready}(v)
.
This is related to the observations Remark
4.2
:
in the algorithmic world we build things, so things
can’t
happen unless we say they
do
; in the logical world we verify things, so things
can
happen unless we say they
don’t
.

5
Crusader Agreement

5.1
Motivation and presenting the protocol

Remark 5.1
.

In Section
4
we gave a declarative axiomatisation of the Bracha Broadcast protocol and we showed how to prove its correctness properties by axiomatic reasoning.

We will now do the same for a Crusader Agreement algorithm as presented in an online exposition
[
IA22
]
.
This exposition was written for the Decentralized Thoughts website
15
15

15

Decentralized Thoughts
is a well-regarded source of technical commentary on decentralised protocols.
by the authors of a journal paper
[
ABDY22
]
, to provide an accessible introduction to their paper.

Agreement is arguably a moderate step up in complexity from Broadcast, because:

(1)

With Broadcast, we want all correct participants to agree on a value broadcast by a single designated (possibly faulty) broadcasting participant.

(2)

With Agreement, we want all correct participants to agree on some value, where all participants start off with their own individual values.
While still simple, it is slightly more challenging in the sense that correct participants can propose different values whereas in Broadcast they cannot.

Definition 17
.

(1)

Figure
8
presents the pseudocode from
[
IA22
]
.

(2)

Figure
9
presents
ThyCA
, a corresponding declarative specification in axiomatic style.

Remark 5.2
.

The authors of
[
IA22
]
are not completely explicit, but it becomes clear from context, that:

(1)

Clauses in Figure
8
are intended to be executed in parallel (not necessarily sequentially).

(2)

Each participant starts with their own input value, which may be different from the input value(s) of other participants.

Intuitively, in this algorithm participants try to agree participants on a value
0
or
1
, and if a participant can see no clear choice of value to agree on, then it might output a special ‘agreement failed’ value.
Here we write this value as
{0.5}
; in
[
IA22
]
they write
{\boldsymbol{\bot}}
(a riff on the domain-theoretic ‘undefined’ element), but that clashes with our use in this paper of
{\boldsymbol{\bot}}
for logical falsity in Figure
3
.

1
input: v (0 or 1)

2
send <echo1, v> to all

3
if didnt send <echo1, 1-v> and see f+1 <echo1, 1-v>

4
send <echo1, 1-v> to all

5
if didnt send <echo2, *> and see n-f <echo1, w>

6
send <echo2, w>

7
if see n-f <echo2, u> and n-f <echo1, u>

8
output(u)

9
if see n-f <echo1, 0> and n-f <echo1, 1>

10
output(0.5)

Figure 8:
Pseudocode Crusader Agreement algorithm from
[
IA22
]
(Definition
17
)

\begin{array}[t]{l@{\quad}r@{\ }l}\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\lx@text@underline{\emph{Backward rules}}\hss}}\\
{\mathsf{(CaEcho1?)}}&&\mathsf{echo}_{1}(a)\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(a)\\
{\mathsf{(CaEcho2?)}}&&\mathsf{echo}_{2}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(a)\\
{\mathsf{(CaOutput?)}}&&(\mathsf{output}(0)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(0))\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}(\mathsf{output}(1)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(1))\\
{\mathsf{(CaOutput^{\prime}?)}}&&\mathsf{output}(0.5)\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(0)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(1))\\
\\
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\lx@text@underline{\emph{Other rules}}\hss}}\\
{\mathsf{(CaCorrect)}}&&\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{input},\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{echo}_{2},\mathsf{output}]\\
{\mathsf{(CaCorrect^{\prime})}}&&\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{P}]\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{P}]\quad(\mathsf{P}{\in}\{\mathsf{input},\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{echo}_{2},\mathsf{output}\})\\
{\mathsf{(CaInput)}}&&\bigl((\mathsf{input}(0)\oplus\mathsf{input}(1))\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{\neg}}\mathsf{input}(0.5)\bigr)\\
{\mathsf{(CaEcho2_{01})}}&&{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists_{01}$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.\mathsf{echo}_{2}(a)\\
\\
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\lx@text@underline{\emph{Forward rules}}\hss}}\\
{\mathsf{(CaEcho1!)}}&&(\mathsf{input}(a)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(a))\mathbin{\rightarrow}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(a)\\
{\mathsf{(CaEcho2!)}}&&{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a.\mathsf{echo}_{2}(a)\\
{\mathsf{(CaOutput!)}}&&\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\mathsf{output}(a)\\
{\mathsf{(CaOutput^{\prime}!)}}&&(\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(0)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(1))\mathbin{\rightarrow}\mathsf{output}(0.5)\end{array}

As standard, free
a
above are assumed universally quantified (i.e. there is an invisible
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}a
at the start of any axiom with a free
a
).
The axioms above assume predicate symbols
\mathcal{P}=\{\mathsf{input},\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{echo}_{2},\mathsf{output}\}
(as explained in Definition
10
).

Figure 9:
ThyCA
: axioms of Crusader Agreement (Definition

)

\begin{array}[t]{l@{\quad}r@{\ }l@{\qquad}l}{\mathsf{(CaAgree)}}&&\text{ThyCA}\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}(\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime}))\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}v{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v^{\prime}&\text{(Proposition~\ref{prop.ca.weak.agreement})}\\

Figure 10:
Correctness properties for
ThyCA
(Definition
18
)

Definition 18
.

Relevant correctness properties for Crusader Agreement as per
[
IA22
]
are presented in Figure
10
.
We consider them in turn:

(1)

Weak Agreement:

If two non-faulty parties output values
v
and
v^{\prime}
, then either
v=v^{\prime}
or one of the values is
{0.5}
.

This becomes
\text{ThyCA}\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}(\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime}))\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}v{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v^{\prime}
.
See Proposition
19
.

(2)

Validity:

If all non-faulty parties have the same input, then this is the only possible output
of any non-faulty party
.
16
16

16

I added the ‘for any non-faulty party’ (the original source material
[
IA22
]
did not say this) because from context it is clear that this is what is intended. Faulty parties are not constrained.

Furthermore, if a non-faulty party outputs
v\neq{0.5}
, then
v
was the input of some non-faulty party.

These become

\text{ThyCA}\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}(\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime}))\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}v{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v^{\prime}

and

\text{ThyCA}\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
.
See Proposition
20
.

(3)

Liveness:

If all non-faulty parties start the protocol then all non-faulty parties eventually output a value.

This becomes
\text{ThyCA}\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.\mathsf{output}(v)
.
See Proposition
21
.

5.2
Discussion of the axioms

Remark 5.3
.

As for the axioms in Figures
2
and
6
, the axioms in Figure
9
are grouped into
backward rules
with names ending in
?
which represent reasoning of the form “if
this
happens, then
that
must have happened”;
forward rules
have with ending with
!
which represent forward reasoning of the form “if
this
happens, then
that
must happen”; and
other rules
which reflect particular conditions of the algorithms.

Recall that each axiom is implicitly universally quantified over
a
if required, and it is valid of a model when it is valid (i.e. it returns
{\mathbf{t}}
or
{\mathbf{b}}
, but not
{\mathbf{f}}
) when evaluated at every
p\in\mathsf{P}
.

Remark 5.4
.

We discuss each axiom in Figure
2
in turn:

(1)

{\mathsf{(CaEcho1?)}}
says: if
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)
is
{\mathbf{t}}
at
p
then
\mathsf{input}(v)
is
{\mathbf{t}}
at some
p^{\prime}
.

This axiom does not immediately correspond to lines 2 to 4 in Figure
8
— contrast with
{\mathsf{(CaEcho!)}}
, which more clearly corresponds to the algorithm.
We explain and discuss this difference below in Remark
5.5
.

(2)

{\mathsf{(CaEcho2?)}}
says: if
\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v)
is
{\mathbf{t}}
at
p
then for a quorum of participants
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)
is
{\mathbf{t}}
or
{\mathbf{b}}
.
This corresponds to part of lines 5 and 6, since it is clear that if a participant performs
\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v)
then it must have seen a quorum of
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)
.

The condition
if didnt send <echo2, *>
on line 5 is captured by
{\mathsf{(CaEcho2_{01})}}
, which just asserts that no correct participant may
echo2

twice
.

(3)

{\mathsf{(CaOutput?)}}
and
{\mathsf{(CaOutput!)}}
reflect lines 7 to 10,
except for one detail: lines 7 to 10 suggest a pair of axioms

\mathsf{output}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(a)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(a))\qquad(\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(a)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(a))\mathbin{\rightarrow}\mathsf{output}(a)

but instead in Figure
9
we have axioms

\mathsf{output}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(a)\qquad\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(a)\mathbin{\rightarrow}\mathsf{output}(a).

Why the difference?

Because, in fact, the right-hand conjunct in the algorithm from
[
IA22
]
is redundant.
17
17

17

This became clear while doing the axiomatic proofs; the right-hand conjunct was never used. In fact the same is true in the source article, but this is harder to spot because its reasoning is less explicitly axiomatic.

We could include it in the axioms and it would do no harm, but it also makes no difference to the proofs and would take up space.

One of the algorithm’s authors
18
18

18

Ittai Abraham; private correspondence.
confirms this: the right-hand conjunct was left in by accident.
It gives stronger correctness properties that are treated in the paper
[
ABDY22
]
, but they are not mentioned in the (simplified) online article
[
IA22
]
.

(4)

{\mathsf{(CaOutput^{\prime}?)}}
asserts that if we output
0.5
then we must have seen quorums of
\mathsf{echo}_{1}
for both
0
and
1
, as per lines 9 and 10 in Figure
8
.

(5)

{\mathsf{(CaCorrect)}}
asserts that there exists a quorum of correct participants, by which we mean precisely that that they all return
{\mathbf{t}}
or
{\mathbf{f}}
truth-values for
\mathsf{input}
,
\mathsf{echo}_{1}
,
\mathsf{echo}_{2}
, and
\mathsf{output}
, and they never return
{\mathbf{b}}
.

(6)

{\mathsf{(CaCorrect^{\prime})}}
asserts that
either
a participant is correct and always returns correct truth-values —
or
it always returns the faulty truth-value
{\mathbf{b}}
.

The reader might ask why a faulty participant must always return
{\mathbf{b}}
; what if it returns
{\mathbf{b}}
just sometimes?
The reason is that this logically captures worst-case behaviour.
Specifically, the forward and backward rules are all implications, and it is a fact that if the left-hand side of an implication is
{\mathbf{b}}
, then the implication overall returns
{\mathbf{b}}
or
{\mathbf{t}}
and so is valid; the reader can check this in Figure
1
, just by observing that the row for
{\mathbf{b}}
in the truth-tables for
\mathbin{\rightarrow}
and
\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}
is ‘
{\mathbf{t}}\ {\mathbf{b}}\ {\mathbf{b}}
’ and this does not mention
{\mathbf{f}}
.

So, this makes
{\mathbf{b}}
behave like a ‘worst case truth-value’ in the sense that all forward and backward rules are valid if the left-hand side returns
{\mathbf{b}}
, so that in effect the rule does not constrain behaviour involving faulty participants.
But, we do not need to keep saying ‘for a non-faulty participant’, because the three-valued logic takes care of all this for us, automatically.

(7)

{\mathsf{(CaInput)}}
expresses line 1: a correct participant inputs either
0
or
1
.

(8)

{\mathsf{(CaEcho1!)}}
expresses the forward version of lines 2 to 4; echo1 the value of your input and echo1 the value of any contraquorum that you see.

(9)

{\mathsf{(CaEcho2!)}}
expresses the forward version of lines 5 and 6.
We need the existential quantifier because in this case, we only echo2 the value of the first quorum of echo1 that we see.

(10)

{\mathsf{(CaOutput!)}}
was discussed above.
We would just add here that
\mathsf{echo}_{2}(0.5)
is impossible by Lemma
5.22
(
3
): that is, in the presence of the other axioms, we do not need an explicit side-condition that
a\neq 0.5
because that turns out to be a lemma.

(11)

{\mathsf{(CaOutput^{\prime}!)}}
corresponds directly to lines 9 and 10 in Figure
8
(and is just the reverse of
{\mathsf{(CaOutput^{\prime}?)}}
).

Remark 5.5
.

{\mathsf{(CaEcho?)}}
is rather special.
A direct reading lines 2 and 3 of the algorithm from Definition
17
suggests we write the following property:

\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)\mathbin{\rightarrow}(\mathsf{input}_{1}(v)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)).

Indeed, the forward rule
{\mathsf{(CaEcho!)}}
looks just like that, just in the other direction.
So why did we not write the predicate above as our
{\mathsf{(CaEcho?)}}
?

Because it is subtly too weak.
The axiom above would permit a model in which (for example) all participants are correct, and they all input
0
, but they all perform
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(1)
.
Note that
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(1)\mathbin{\rightarrow}{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(1)
is valid of this model.
Clearly we want to exclude it, and no practical run of the algorithm would generate it, but logically speaking it perfectly well satisfies the restriction above.

What is going on here is that our model has no notion of time, so our model has no notion of a ‘first’
\mathsf{echo}_{1}
that would need to be justified by an input.

We could now be literal and introduce a notion of time, either as an explicit timestamp parameter to each
\mathsf{echo}_{1}
, or we could add a notion of time to the modal context in the logic itself.
There would be nothing wrong with that — it reflects what the algorithm actually does and is technically entirely feasible.

But we will prefer a simpler, more general, and arguably more elegant approach.
19
19

19

But as always: what you axiomatise depends on what you want to represent. Today, we might make one design decision about how much detail we want to include in our model. Tomorrow, or for a different protocol, or for a different intended audience, it would be perfectly legitimate to choose different tradeoffs.

To explain how this works, we reason as follows:
we observe of the algorithm that if a correct participant performs
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)
then either
it
has input
v
, or
f{+}1
participants — and thus in particular one correct participant — also have performed
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)
.
By an induction on time, we see that if a correct participant performs
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)
then some some correct participant has input
v
.
This justifies writing
{\mathsf{(CaEcho?)}}
as we do in Figure
9
.

It is not immediately obvious that this suffices to prove our correctness properties; we have to check that in the proofs.
And, it turns out that the proofs work well, which gives a formal sense in which the
{\mathsf{(CaEcho?)}}
axiom in Figure
9
is correct and in fact, it is more general than the more literal rendering.
20
20

20

Why is a weaker axiom better? Surely strong axioms with lots of structure are good? No: generally speaking, the game is to derive strong and well-structured correctness properties from weak and minimally-structured axioms; our game is to derive the strongest possible theorems from the weakest possible assumptions.

5.3
Proofs of correctness properties

5.3.1
Weak Agreement

Remark 5.6
.

The proof of Proposition
19
is straightforward.
Note how the ‘non-faulty’ precondition is integrated into the use of
\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}
, which assumes that the left-hand side returns
{\mathbf{t}}
(the non-faulty truth-value).
21
21

21

On the right-hand side,
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[v{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v^{\prime}]\hskip-1.99997pt]}\in\{{\mathbf{t}},{\mathbf{f}}\}
, so equality always returns a correct truth-value.

Proposition 19

(Weak Agreement)
.

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\text{ThyCA}
and
v,v^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.
Then

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}(\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime}))\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}v{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v^{\prime}.

This formalises the following informal statement:

“If two non-faulty parties output values
v
and
v^{\prime}
, then either
v=v^{\prime}
or one of the values is
{0.5}
”

Proof 5.7
.

Using strong modus ponens (Prop.
2
(
5
)) it suffices to assume
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime})
and prove
v=v^{\prime}
.

So suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime})
.
By
{\mathsf{(CaOutput?)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v^{\prime})
.
By
{\mathsf{(CaCorrect)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{2}]
.
By Theorem
2.13
and Lemma
2.10
(
3
)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v^{\prime}))
.
By
{\mathsf{(CaEcho2_{01})}}
and Proposition
9
(
1
.
1a
&
1
.
1e
)
v=v^{\prime}
as required.

Example 5.8
.

\mathsf{output}
is not functional, by which we mean that
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists_{01}$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.\mathsf{output}(v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}
need not be equal to
{\mathbf{t}}
.
We give an example to show that is possible to have
p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{output}(0.5)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{output}(1))
.

Consider a model with an even number of participants; all participants are correct; and half of them input
0
and the other half input
1
.
The reader can check that every participant necessarily produces
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(0)
and
\mathsf{echo}_{1}(1)
, so that all participants
\mathsf{output}(0.5)
.
It is also consistent with the axioms for every participant to produce
\mathsf{echo}_{2}(1)
and
\mathsf{output}(1)
.

5.3.2
Validity

We work towards Proposition
20
; we will need Lemma
5.9
:

Lemma 5.9
.

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\text{ThyCA}
and
v\in\{0,0.5,1\}
and
p\in\mathsf{P}
.
Then:

(1)

p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{input}(v)
if and only if
p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}{\boldsymbol{\neg}}\mathsf{input}(1{\text{-}}v)
.

(2)

p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
and
p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v^{\prime})
implies
v=v^{\prime}
.

(3)

p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
and
p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v^{\prime})
implies
v=v^{\prime}
.

Proof 5.10
.

We consider each part in turn:

(1)

We consider three subcases:

•

Suppose
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{input}(v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)={\mathbf{b}}
.

Then by
{\mathsf{(CaCorrect^{\prime})}}
(for
\mathsf{input}
)
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{input}(v^{\prime})]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)={\mathbf{b}}
for
every

v^{\prime}\in\{0,0.5.1\}
and in particular
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{input}(1{\text{-}}v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)={\mathbf{b}}={\boldsymbol{\neg}}{\mathbf{b}}
so we are done.

•

Suppose
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{input}(v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)={\mathbf{t}}
.

Then by
{\mathsf{(CaCorrect^{\prime})}}
(for
\mathsf{input}
)
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{input}(v^{\prime})]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)\in\{{\mathbf{t}},{\mathbf{f}}\}
for
every

v^{\prime}\in\{0,0.5.1\}
and in particular
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{input}(1{\text{-}}v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)\in\{{\mathbf{t}},{\mathbf{f}}\}
.
By
{\mathsf{(CaInput_{1})}}
and Proposition
9
(
1
.
1e
),
\mathsf{input}(v)
is true for precisely
one
value
v\in\{0,1\}
.
The result follows.

•

The case that
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{input}(v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)={\mathbf{f}}
is precisely symmetric to the case of
{\mathbf{t}}
.

(2)

By routine logical reasoning from part
1
of this result.

(3)

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
and
p\in\mathsf{P}
and
p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v^{\prime})
.
Then
p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{input}(v)
and
p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v^{\prime})
.
By
{\mathsf{(CaCorrect^{\prime})}}
(for
\mathsf{input}
) and Proposition
2
(
8
),
p\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
.
We use part
2
of this result.

Proposition 20

(Validity)
.

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\text{ThyCA}
and
v\in\{0,1\}
(so
v\neq 0.5
).
Then:

(1)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
.

“If a non-faulty party outputs
v\neq{0.5}
, then
v
was the input of some non-faulty party.”

(2)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
and
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime})
implies
v{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v^{\prime}
, for every
v^{\prime}\in\{0,0.5,1\}
.

“If all non-faulty parties have the same input, then this is the only possible output
of any non-faulty party
.”

Proof 5.11
.

We consider each part in turn:

(1)

By strong modus ponens (Prop.
2
(
5
)) it suffices to show that
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)
implies
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
.

So suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)
.
By
{\mathsf{(CaOutput?)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v)
and by
{\mathsf{(CaCorrect)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{2}]
.
Using Theorem
2.13
and Lemma
2.10
(
3
) (or just direct from the 3-twined property in Definition
5
)
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{echo}_{2}(v)
.

By similar reasoning using
{\mathsf{(CaEcho2?)}}
in place of
{\mathsf{(CaOutput?)}}
, we conclude that
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)
.
By
{\mathsf{(CaEcho1?)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
as required.

(2)

Suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
and suppose
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime})
; we will show
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt(v{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v^{\prime})
, i.e.
v=v^{\prime}
.

By part
1
of this result
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v^{\prime})
.
But we assumed
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
, so by Lemma
5.9
(
3
)
v=v^{\prime}
as required.

Remark 5.12
.

We take a minute to discuss the precise form of the validity properties in Proposition
20
.
Consider this English sentence, where
v\neq{0.5}
:

“If a non-faulty party outputs
v\neq{0.5}
, then
v
was the input of some non-faulty party.”

We render it as

\text{ThyCA}\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{output}(v)\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v).

By strong modus ponens (Prop.
2
(
5
)), it means that
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{output}(v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p)={\mathbf{t}}
for some
p\in\mathsf{P}
implies
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\mathsf{input}(v)]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{\mu}(p^{\prime})={\mathbf{t}}
for some
p^{\prime}\in\mathsf{P}
.
We are using
{\mathbf{t}}
and
{\mathbf{f}}
as the truth-values of correct (non-faulty) participants, and
{\mathbf{b}}
as the truth-value for incorrect (faulty) participants, so this corresponds precisely to the English sentence quoted above.

It might be instructive to consider a subtly incorrect rendering of

“If all non-faulty parties have the same input, then this is the only possible output
of any non-faulty party
”

as

\text{ThyCA}\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{input}(v)\mathbin{\rightharpoonup}\mathsf{output}(v).

This is wrong, for two reasons:

(1)

\mathsf{output}
is just a predicate-symbol; it is not a function-symbol.
As such,
\mathsf{output}(v)
does not
a priori
have to return
{\mathbf{t}}
or
{\mathbf{b}}
on just one value
v
.
22
22

22

Indeed, in the case of this particular protocol
\mathsf{output}
can be
{\mathbf{t}}
on two values; see Example
5.8
.

\mathsf{output}(v)
could be
{\mathbf{t}}
and also
\mathsf{output}(v^{\prime})
might be
{\mathbf{t}}
for some other
v^{\prime}
.

(2)

More subtly, just because
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{input}(v)
holds does not
a priori
mean that
\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}{\mathbf{t}}\Diamond\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(1{\text{-}}v)
cannot hold.
Remember that we are working over an abstract 3-twined semitopology; there might be a quorum of correct participants who
{\mathbf{t}}
-do
\mathsf{input}(v)
and
also
some other correct participant who
{\mathbf{t}}
-does
\mathsf{input}(v^{\prime})
for some other
v^{\prime}
.

This is why we write
\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.5pt\Box\hskip 1.5pt\mathsf{input}(v)
in
{\mathsf{(CaValid1)}}
in Figure
10
: it means that every participant is either faulty or, if it is not faulty, it inputs
v
.
The use of exclusive-or
\oplus
in
{\mathsf{(CaInput)}}
ensures that
\mathsf{input}
is functional, so this does indeed render the idea of the English sentence quoted above.

We work towards Proposition

.
We will need Lemma

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(v)

.

.

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\text{ThyCA}

Then:

(1)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}(\mathsf{input}(0)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1}])\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}(\mathsf{input}(1)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1}])
.

(2)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(0)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(1)
.

(3)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(0)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{echo}_{1}(1)
.

(4)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{echo}_{2}(0)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(1))
.

(5)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}(\mathsf{echo}_{2}(0)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{echo}_{2}(1))
.

Proof 5.18
.

We consider each part in turn:

(1)

Using
{\mathsf{(CaInput)}}
and
{\mathsf{(CaCorrect)}}

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\Box\hskip 1.5pt(\mathsf{input}(0)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{input}(1))\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{input}]
, so by Lemma
2.8

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}((\mathsf{input}(0)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{input}(1))\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{input}])
,
and rearranging we obtain

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\raisebox{0.7pt}{${\mathchoice{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{\square}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\square}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}$}((\mathsf{input}(0)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{input}])\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}(\mathsf{input}(1)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{input}])).

By Corollary
2.15
(since we assume
(\mathsf{P},\mathsf{Open})
is 3-twined)

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}(\mathsf{input}(0)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{input}])\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}(\mathsf{input}(1)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1},\mathsf{input}]).

By routine calculations from Figure
1
we conclude that

\vDash_{\hskip-2.0pt\mu}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}(\mathsf{input}(0)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1}])\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathchoice{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}{\vphantom{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{{\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{1.35}[1]{$\lozenge$}}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\cr}}}}(\mathsf{input}(1)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 1.5pt\hskip-2.0pt[\mathsf{echo}_{1}])

as required.

(2)

We combine part
1
of this result with
{\mathsf{(CaEcho1!)}}
(left-hand disjunct, for
\mathsf{input}
) using weak modus ponens and Proposition
2
(
8
).

From part
2
of this result using Lemma

.

From part

of this result using

.

From part
